const p=String.raw`
section(5, 'Discretizing the heat equation')
task('5.1', 'One dimensional update')
body('(a) For an interior node, use the second difference in the method of lines. The prescribed Dirichlet values supply the end nodes.')
eq('du_{i}/dt = α[u_{i−1} − 2u_{i} + u_{i+1}]/Δx².')
body('(b–c) Forward Euler evaluates that right-hand side at time level n. The dimensionless parameter is r = αΔt/Δx².')
eq('u^{n+1}_{i} = (1 − 2r)u^{n}_{i} + r(u^{n}_{i−1} + u^{n}_{i+1}).')
body('The combination is the same as αt/L² in Task 1.2, now using one time step and one grid spacing.')

task('5.2', 'Two dimensional update')
body('(a) The update at an interior node adds αΔt times the two spatial second differences from Task 3.4, each evaluated at time level n.')
eq('u^{n+1}(i,j) = u^{n}(i,j) + αΔt[D_{xx}u^{n}(i,j) + D_{yy}u^{n}(i,j)].')
body('(b) For equal spacing h, set r = αΔt/h². The center weight is 1 − 4r and each neighbor has weight r.')
eq('u^{n+1}(i,j) = (1 − 4r)u^{n}(i,j) + r[u^{n}(i−1,j) + u^{n}(i+1,j) + u^{n}(i,j−1) + u^{n}(i,j+1)].')
body('(c) The four neighbors and the center come from the old grid. Only the new center value is written.')
code('Old level n                         New level n + 1\n'
     '          Top\n'
     'Left     Center     Right    ────>        Center\n'
     '         Bottom')
body('(d) With the weights precomputed, summing the four neighbors takes three additions. Two multiplications and one final addition give six floating-point operations per update. Each update uses five input grid values and writes one. Cache reuse reduces the number of memory accesses that reach RAM.')

task('5.3', 'Interpreting the weights')
body('(a) The weights sum to (1 − 4r) + 4r = 1. If the five input values all equal C, the new value remains C. A uniform plate stays uniform when the boundaries are compatible.')
body('(b) The change equals 4r times the four-neighbor average minus the old center value. A warmer neighborhood raises the center temperature. A cooler neighborhood lowers it.')

task('5.4', 'Insulated edges')
body('(a) The left-edge nodes u(0,j) with 1 ≤ j ≤ Nᵧ − 1 are unknowns and are updated. Add one ghost column at x = −Δx. Centering the zero-derivative condition at the wall gives u(−1,j) = u(1,j) at every step.')
body('(b) At a mixed corner, the prescribed Dirichlet value fixes the corner. At a corner joining two insulated edges, the corner is unknown. Mirror the two off-grid cardinal neighbors across their respective walls, then apply the five-point update to the corner.')
body('(c) If the prescribed coordinate derivative is uₓ(0,y,t) = h(t), the centered boundary equation and ghost value are')
eq('[u(1,j) − u(−1,j)]/(2Δx) = h(t),     u(−1,j) = u(1,j) − 2Δx h(t).')

task('5.5', 'Truncation error and consistency', page=True)
body('Substitute the smooth exact solution into the discrete equation. Define τ as the forward time difference minus α times the sum of the centered space differences. Taylor expansion gives')
eq('Forward time difference = u_{t} + (Δt/2)u_{tt} + O(Δt²).')
eq('D_{xx}u = u_{xx} + (Δx²/12)u_{xxxx} + O(Δx⁴).')
eq('D_{yy}u = u_{yy} + (Δy²/12)u_{yyyy} + O(Δy⁴).')
body('The PDE cancels the leading time and space terms. The leading remainder is')
eq('τ = (Δt/2)u_{tt} − (αΔx²/12)u_{xxxx} − (αΔy²/12)u_{yyyy} + O(Δt² + Δx⁴ + Δy⁴).')
body('Thus τ = O(Δt) + O(Δx²) + O(Δy²), so p = 1 and q = 2. The truncation error vanishes as all step sizes shrink. The method is consistent.')

section(6, 'Stability and convergence', page=False)
task('6.1', 'Upwind amplification factor')
body('For the upwind update, G = 1 − ν + ν exp(−iθ) and its conjugate has exp(iθ). Expand their product.')
eq('|G|² = (1−ν)² + ν² + 2ν(1−ν) cos θ.')
eq('        = 1 − 2ν(1−ν)(1−cos θ) = 1 − 4ν(1−ν) sin²(θ/2).')
body('Therefore all modes satisfy |G| ≤ 1 exactly when 0 ≤ ν ≤ 1.')

task('6.2', 'Von Neumann analysis in one dimension')
body('(a) For the mode exp(ijθ) at grid point j, the two neighbors contribute factors exp(±iθ). The amplification factor is real.')
eq('G(θ) = 1 − 2r + 2r cos θ = 1 − 4r sin²(θ/2).')
body('(b) G ranges from 1 − 4r to 1 for r ≥ 0. The zero-frequency mode has |G| = 1. The mode θ = π, with values alternating as (−1)ʲ, has the smallest G and sets the stability limit. It has the largest magnitude if r > 1/2 and ties the zero mode at r = 1/2.')
body('(c) Require 1 − 4r ≥ −1. This gives r ≤ 1/2, or Δt ≤ Δx²/(2α).')

task('6.3', 'Von Neumann analysis in two dimensions')
body('(a) In the mode exp[i(jθ + kφ)], shifts in x and y contribute exp(±iθ) and exp(±iφ). Pairing opposite directions gives')
eq('G = 1 − 4r_{x} sin²(θ/2) − 4r_{y} sin²(φ/2),    r_{x} = αΔt/Δx²,    r_{y} = αΔt/Δy².')
body('(b) The minimum is at θ = φ = π, the checkerboard mode. The maximum is 1. Requiring the minimum to be at least −1 gives')
eq('αΔt(1/Δx² + 1/Δy²) ≤ 1/2.')
body('(c–d) For equal spacing, the bound is r ≤ 1/4 in 2D. Each direction can contribute −4r to G, giving the limits 1/2 in 1D and 1/6 in 3D.')

task('6.4', 'Nonnegative weights and temperature bounds', page=True)
body('The four neighbor weights are the two nonnegative directional values rₓ and rᵧ. The center weight is 1 − 2rₓ − 2rᵧ. All weights are nonnegative when 2(rₓ + rᵧ) ≤ 1, the same bound as Task 6.3. With equal spacing this is r ≤ 1/4.')
body('The weights sum to 1, so under the bound the update is a weighted average. If the center weight is negative, the update can create temperatures outside the old range. For example, a center value 1 surrounded by zeros would become negative.')

task('6.6', 'Counting the work')
body('(a) The grid contains (N+1)² nodes, of which (N−1)² are updated. For α = 1 and h = 1/N, the largest stable step is 1/(4N²). Reaching T = 0.1 takes K = ⌈0.4N²⌉ steps, with the step shortened slightly when needed to reach T exactly.')
body('(b–c) At six operations per interior update, the work is 6K(N−1)² ≈ 2.4N⁴ operations. Doubling N therefore increases the work and the estimated runtime by about 16.')
table(('N','Steps K','Floating-point operations','Time at 10⁹ operations/s'),[
    (f'{n:,}', f'{(2*n*n+4)//5:,}', sci(6*((2*n*n+4)//5)*(n-1)**2), time)
    for n,time in ((1000,'2,395 s (39.9 min)'),(4000,'614,093 s (7.11 days)'))
],(.68,.98,2.16,2.60), '(d) Estimates based on the six-operation stencil count.')

task('6.7', 'Deliberate instability')
body('The Part 1 Case V run used N = 16 and r = 0.2625, 5% above the limit. The ideal alternating mode has G = 1 − 8r = −1.1. For the finite grid, the highest sine mode has nearly the same checkerboard shape and still has |G| > 1. Its sign flips each step while its amplitude grows.')
body('The error was small at 300 steps. At 400 steps the oscillations are most visible in the plate interior near the center. By 500 steps the error exceeds 1. The snapshots locate the early visible pattern without identifying an exact onset step.')
instability = []
for steps in (100,300,400,500,600,1000):
    log = (results/f'instability_{steps}.txt').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
    err = float(re.search(r'^Max error = (\S+)',log,re.M)[1])
    instability.append((steps,err))
table(('Steps','Max. error','Steps','Max. error'),[
    (f'{a[0]:,}',sci(a[1]),f'{b[0]:,}',sci(b[1]))
    for a,b in zip(instability[:3],instability[3:])
],(1.05,1.85,1.05,1.85), 'Saved maximum absolute errors.')
figure(intermediate_plot,4.93,'Error at 400 and 500 steps. The panels use different color scales.',
       'Two alternating error patterns with the strongest oscillations near the center.')
figure(instability_plot,2.67,'Final temperature field after 1,000 unstable steps.',
       'Checkerboard heat map after 1,000 steps showing alternating large positive and negative temperatures.')
`;
store("num_part_c",p);text(p.length);

const p=String.raw`
section(7, 'Verifying the solver')
task('7.1', 'Expected convergence order')
body('Task 5.5 gives a time term of order Δt and a space term of order h². With r fixed, Δt = rh²/α, so both terms scale as h². For this stable, smooth case we expect order two in h. Halving h should reduce the error by about four.')

task('7.2', 'Case V convergence study')
body('(a) The serial solver used Case V with α = 1, T = 0.05, and r = 0.2 at N = 16, 32, 64, 128, and 256. The step count T/(rh²) was an integer on each grid.')
body('(b) At the final time, compare the numerical field with sin(πx) sin(πy) exp(−2π²T). We measure the maximum nodal error and the discrete L₂ error, which is h times the square root of the sum of squared nodal errors.')
with (results/'convergence.csv').open(newline='',encoding='utf-8') as fp:
    convergence = list(csv.DictReader(fp))
table(('N','h','Steps','Maximum error','Discrete L₂ error','Observed order'),[
    (row['N'],f"1/{row['N']}",f"{int(row['steps']):,}",sci(float(row['max_error']),5),
     sci(float(row['l2h_error']),5),f"{float(row['p_obs']):.4f}" if row['p_obs'] else '—')
    for row in convergence
],(.54,.62,.81,1.55,1.63,1.35),'Saved serial results at T = 0.05.')
figure(convergence_plot,6.35,'Log-log plot of the two errors against h. The reference line has slope 2.',
       'The maximum error and the discrete L2 error both decline with approximately second-order slope.')
body('(c) Using p = log₂[E(h)/E(h/2)] for the discrete L₂ error, the measured order changes from 2.0058 to 2.0001 across the refinements. This agrees with Task 7.1.')

section(8, 'From the scheme to MPI')
task('8.1', 'Why two time levels are needed')
body('(a) In a row-by-row sweep with increasing i and j, an interior point’s left and bottom neighbors have already been visited and contain level n + 1. Its right and top neighbors still contain level n. Fixed boundary values stay fixed.')
body('(b) The five-point forward Euler formula requires all input values at level n. Overwriting values in place instead mixes levels, and reversing the sweep changes which neighbors have already been updated.')
body('(c) With MPI, the mixed time levels could also depend on rank ownership and the order in which halos arrive. Separate old and new arrays let every rank read only level n, including its halos, and write only level n + 1. Swapping the arrays preserves the serial calculation for every partition.')

task('8.2', 'Surface to volume and halo costs')
body('Use the ideal N × N point grid and a typical interior rank. The square-block case assumes a square process grid. Each rank updates about N²/P values per step.')
table(('Decomposition','Values sent','Values received','Messages sent / received'),[
    ('(a) Strips','2N','2N','2 / 2'),
    ('(b) Square blocks','4N/√P','4N/√P','4 / 4'),
], (1.75,1.22,1.25,2.32), 'Per interior rank per time step.')
body('A strip has two neighboring rows of N values. A square block has four sides of N/√P values. The five-point stencil uses only these row and column halos. A stencil with diagonal terms, such as the nine-point stencil, would also need the corner values.')
body('(c) With a startup cost tₛ and a transfer cost per value, budget one message cost per neighbor, treating the paired send and receive as one exchange in this simple estimate.')
eq('T_{strips} ≈ 2t_{s} + 2N t_{w},     T_{blocks} ≈ 4t_{s} + (4N/√P)t_{w}.')
body('When latency is small, blocks have the smaller data volume for P > 4. When latency dominates, strips benefit from fewer messages. The interior-rank model favors blocks when the startup cost satisfies the inequality below.')
eq('t_{s} < N t_{w}(1 − 2/√P).')
body('(d) If γ is the computation time per update, the strip computation cost is about γN²/P. The ratio becomes')
eq('T_{comm}/T_{comp} ≈ 2P t_{s}/(γN²) + 2P t_{w}/(γN).')
body('At fixed P the latency term falls like 1/N², and the transfer term falls like 1/N. Larger grids offer more computation per exchanged value, which makes parallelism easier to justify on a slow network.')

heading('Application to the measured MPI cluster',page=True)
body('The project uses N intervals, so it updates (N−1)² interior points. In strips, the N−1 rows are split as evenly as possible. One actual row message contains N+1 doubles. For example, at N = 16 and P = 3, each rank owns five interior rows and sends 17 doubles per neighboring rank. The middle rank has two neighbors and each end rank has one.')

body('For the project’s nonperiodic blocks, count only existing neighbors. Rows have approximate length N divided by the process-grid width, and columns have length N divided by its height. The busiest ranks give the following approximate outgoing costs.')
table(('Configuration','Messages','Values sent','Communication cost per step'),[
    ('Strips, P ≥ 3','2','2N','2tₛ + 2Nt_w'),
    ('Blocks, P = 4','2','N','2tₛ + Nt_w'),
    ('Blocks, P = 6','3','4N/3','3tₛ + (4N/3)t_w'),
    ('Blocks, P = 8','3','5N/4','3tₛ + (5N/4)t_w'),
],(1.46,1.02,1.13,2.89))
body('Using the Part 0 heat-2 to heat-3 ping-pong fit gives 5.7368 ms startup and 4.2975 MB/s effective bandwidth. An eight-byte double then costs about 1.8615 µs to transfer. The table uses the same link cost for every exchange as a simplifying assumption.')

with (root/'part0/results/network_summary.csv').open(newline='',encoding='utf-8') as fp:
    link = next(r for r in csv.DictReader(fp) if r['connection']=='heat2_heat3_direct')
ts = float(link['latency_ms'])
tw = 1000*8/(float(link['bandwidth_MB_s'])*1e6)
network_rows = []
for processes, msgs, length in ((4,2,1), (8,3,1.25)):
    for n in (512,2048,8192):
        strips = 2*ts + 2*n*tw
        blocks = msgs*ts + length*n*tw
        network_rows.append((processes, f'{n:,}', f'{strips:.3f}', f'{blocks:.3f}', 'Blocks' if blocks < strips else 'Strips'))
table(('P','N','Strips (ms)','Blocks (ms)','Predicted faster'),network_rows,
      (.62,.87,1.35,1.35,1.98), 'Part 3 predictions for one step.')

def bench_median(method, n):
    folder = root/'part3/results/final_benchmarks'
    vals = [float(re.search(r'\bt_total=(\S+)', p.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))[1])
            for p in folder.glob(f'{method}_N{n}_P4_R*.log')]
    assert len(vals) == 3
    return median(vals)
table(('N','Strips median (s)','Blocks median (s)','Strips ÷ blocks'), [
    (f'{n:,}',f'{s:.3f}',f'{b:.3f}',f'{s/b:.2f}×')
    for n in (512,2048)
    for s,b in [(bench_median('strips',n),bench_median('blocks',n))]

body('Blocks had the lower measured median at both completed grid sizes, which agrees with the predicted ordering. body('For Part 4, the rank-level model adds the rank’s computation cost to the halo message costs on its own links. The largest estimated rank time approximates the step time. This connects the Part 0 network estimates and Part 4 serial compute rates to the same decomposition model.')

heading('Sources',level=2)
body('Numerical Methods for the Heat Equation, Chapters 1–8. Parallel 2D Heat Diffusion with MPI over Tailscale, Sections 5–8. Part 0 ping-pong fits, Part 1 serial verification logs and figures, and Part 3 final benchmark logs.')

### 🤖 Codex (2026-11-24 19:42:36)

# Part 1 — Serial heat solver

This solver advances the 2D heat equation on the unit square using the explicit five-point stencil and two time-level arrays. It is the serial reference for the MPI strips and blocks solvers.

## Build (Ubuntu / Linux)

```bash
make
```

Run these commands from `part1/`. The default build produces `./heat` with C++17, `-O2`, and `-ffp-contract=off`. The plotting scripts require Python 3 and Matplotlib.

## Run

```bash
… [3120 more characters in the raw .jsonl]

BODY Normal Part 1: Serial heat solver and verification
BODY Normal We implemented and verified a serial solver for heat diffusion on a unit square. The Case V errors decreased by a factor of about four each time the grid spacing was halved. A separate test above the stability limit produced a growing checkerboard pattern.
BODY Heading 1 Numerical method
BODY Normal We use N intervals along each axis, so the spacing is h = 1/N and the grid contains (N + 1)² nodes. Thermal diffusivity is α = 1. At each step, the explicit five-point stencil updates an interior node from its old value and the four adjacent values.
BODY Normal 
BODY Normal 
BODY Normal Here r = Δt/h². The stable solver accepts 0 < r ≤ 0.25. For a requested final time T, it rounds the step count up and divides T by that count so the run reaches T within the requested time-step limit.
BODY Heading 1 Cases and implementation
BODY Normal Cases 1 and 2 start with a cold interior. Case 1 holds all edges at 1. Case 2 holds the top and bottom edges, including their corners, at 1 and the remaining side nodes at 0. Case V starts from sin(πx) sin(πy) with zero edge values.
BODY Normal The C++17 solver stores each time level in a contiguous row-major array. It holds the edges fixed, updates the interior into the second array, and swaps the arrays after each step. The Makefile uses -O2 and -ffp-contract=off. We time only the step loop with std::chrono::steady_clock. The optional binary output stores the full grid as headerless little-endian doubles in row-major order.
BODY Heading 1 Verification
BODY Normal For Cases 1 and 2, the saved checks at N = 8, r = 0.2, and 20 steps confirmed the prescribed edges and temperatures between 0 and 1.
BODY Normal For Case V, the exact solution is sin(πx) sin(πy) exp(−2π²t). At T = 0.05 with r = 0.2, the observed order of the discrete L₂ error approached 2 across N = 16, 32, 64, 128, and 256 (Appendix D). The separate instability run used r = 0.2625, 5% above the limit. The error became visible near the center of the plate and developed into a checkerboard (Appendix E). The verified serial output supplies the reference field for the MPI implementations.
BODY Compact text

CXX = mpic++
CXXFLAGS = -std=c++17 -O2 -ffp-contract=off

all: heat_strips

heat_strips: src/strips.cpp
	$(CXX) $(CXXFLAGS) $< -o $@

clean:
	rm -f heat_strips

.PHONY: all clean
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

mkdir -p results/correctness

passed=0

for N in 8 10 16; do
  for CASE in 1 2 V; do

    SERIAL="results/correctness/serial_${CASE}_${N}.bin"

    ../part1/heat --decomp serial \
      --case "$CASE" --N "$N" \
      --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
      --out "$SERIAL" > /dev/null

    for P in 1 2 3 4; do

      MPI="results/correctness/mpi_${CASE}_${N}_${P}.bin"

      mpirun --oversubscribe -np "$P" ./heat_strips \
        --case "$CASE" --N "$N" \
        --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
        --out "$MPI" > /dev/null

      if cmp -s "$SERIAL" "$MPI"; then
        echo "PASS: Case=$CASE N=$N P=$P"
        passed=$((passed + 1))
      else
        echo "FAIL: Case=$CASE N=$N P=$P"
        exit 1
      fi

    done
  done
done

echo "--------------------------------"
echo "TOTAL: $passed/36 tests passed"

## Distributed Execution over Tailscale

The project uses three Ubuntu VMs:

| VM | Tailscale IP |
|---|---|
| heat-1 | 100.100.125.10 |
| heat-2 | 100.98.24.109 |
| heat-3 | 100.113.165.26 |

All participating VMs must be powered on, connected to Tailscale and have the MPI executable available at the same path.

The solver is compiled on each VM in:

```text
/home/mpi/part2/
```

### Three-VM Hostfile

Create a file named `hosts_3vm`:

```text
100.98.24.109 slots=1
100.100.125.10 slots=1
100.113.165.26 slots=1
```

### Verify Connectivity

```bash
mpirun --hostfile hosts_3vm -np 3 \
  --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl tcp,self \
  hostname
```

The output must contain all three VM hostnames.

### Distributed Case 2

```bash
mpirun --hostfile hosts_3vm -np 3 \
  --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl tcp,self \
  ./heat_strips \
  --decomp strips --case 2 \
  --N 8 --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
  --out results/distributed_case2_N8_P3.bin
```

Generate the serial reference:

```bash
../part1/heat --decomp serial \
  --case 2 --N 8 --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
  --out results/serial_case2_N8_steps10.bin
```

Compare:

```bash
cmp results/serial_case2_N8_steps10.bin \
    results/distributed_case2_N8_P3.bin
```

Case 2 was successfully verified across both two and three VMs.

- P=3

timeout 60s mpirun --hostfile hosts_3vm -np 3 \

1)CASE V: exact solution: near the center sin sin
./heat --decomp serial --case V --N 64 --r 0.2 --T 0.05


Why are t_total and t_comp equal?
Because Part 1 uses one process. The program times the computation loop, and there is no MPI communication inside it.


Why is t_comm=0?
Because no temperatures are exchanged between machines. That communication will be needed in our parallel implementation.


2)CASE 1: edges
./heat --decomp serial --case 1 --N 64 --r 0.2 --steps 100 --out results/demo_case1.bin

t_total and t_comp measure how long the computer takes to execute the simulation, in seconds.

t_total — Total execution time: time spent inside the simulation's time-stepping loop.
t_comp — Computation time: time spent calculating new temperatures using our heat equation:

T	Total simulated time for all steps

In Case 1, all four boundaries are fixed at temperature 1. Heat spreads inward from every direction, creating a hot border around a cold center.

In Case 2, only the top and bottom boundaries are hot. The left and right boundaries remain cold. Therefore, the heatmap shows two hot horizontal regions, while the center stays cold after 100 steps.

Both cases use the same numerical method, grid size and time-step parameter. The different results come entirely from their boundary conditions.

const py=String.raw`
import math
import os
import textwrap
from pathlib import Path
from zipfile import ZipFile
from docx import Document
from docx.enum.text import WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH
from docx.shared import Inches, Pt, RGBColor
from docx.oxml.ns import qn

qa = Path(os.environ['PART1_DEMO_QA'])
repo = Path.cwd()
results = repo/'part1/results'
recorded = next(line for line in (results/'convergence_runs.txt').read_text(encoding='utf-8').splitlines() if ' N=64 ' in line)
fields = dict(item.split('=',1) for item in recorded.split())
assert fields['steps']=='1024' and fields['T']=='0.05' and fields['t_comm']=='0'
assert fields['t_total']==fields['t_comp']
assert math.isclose(100*.2/64**2, .0048828125)
binary_size = (64+1)**2*8

doc = Document()
sec = doc.sections[0]
sec.page_width, sec.page_height = Inches(8.27), Inches(11.69)
sec.left_margin = sec.right_margin = Inches(.73)
sec.top_margin = sec.bottom_margin = Inches(.65)
doc.core_properties.title = 'Part 1 Serial Heat Solver Demo'
doc.core_properties.author = ''
for name, font, size, bold, before, after in [
    ('Normal','Aptos',10.5,False,0,6),
    ('Title','Aptos',17,True,0,9),
    ('Heading 1','Aptos',13,True,9,5),
    ('Heading 2','Aptos',11,True,7,4),
    ('Caption','Aptos',9,False,2,6),
]:
    style = doc.styles[name]
    style.font.name = font
    style.font.size = Pt(size)
    style.font.bold = bold
    style.font.italic = False
    style.font.color.rgb = RGBColor(0,0,0)
    fmt = style.paragraph_format
    fmt.space_before, fmt.space_after = Pt(before), Pt(after)
    fmt.line_spacing = 1.10 if name=='Normal' else 1.02
    fmt.widow_control = True
    if name == 'Title' or name.startswith('Heading'):
        fmt.keep_with_next = True
        fmt.keep_together = True
    props = style.element.get_or_add_pPr()
    for border in props.findall(qn('w:pBdr')):
        props.remove(border)

steps = []
def body(text):
    doc.add_paragraph(text)

def heading(text, page=False, level=1):
    p = doc.add_heading(text, level)
    if page:
        p.paragraph_format.page_break_before = True

def code(text, bash=False):
    p = doc.add_paragraph()
    p.paragraph_format.keep_together = True
    p.paragraph_format.space_after = Pt(7)
    p.paragraph_format.left_indent = Inches(.13)
    p.paragraph_format.line_spacing = 1.02
    run = p.add_run(text)
    run.font.name = 'Consolas'
    run.font.size = Pt(9)
    if bash:
        steps.append(text)

def note(label,text):
    p = doc.add_paragraph()
    p.add_run(label + ' ').bold = True
    p.add_run(text)

doc.add_heading('Part 1 Serial Heat Solver Demo', 0)
body('This demo shows that the serial solver agrees with an exact solution and produces the expected temperature fields for the two boundary cases.')
note('Where to run.', 'Use Bash on the Ubuntu VM heat-2 as the mpi user. Run the steps in the same terminal so the demo_dir variable stays available. The build uses g++ and make.')

heading('1 Build the serial solver')
code('cd ~/part1\n'
     'make\n'
     'demo_dir=$(mktemp -d)', bash=True)
note('Expected.', 'The build creates ./heat. If it is already up to date, make may say there is nothing to do. The temporary directory keeps the new demo files separate from the saved results.')
note('What to say.', '“The solver uses one process and two grids. Each interior point is updated from its old value and its four neighbors. We keep the boundary values fixed and swap grids after each step.”')

heading('2 Verify against the exact solution in Case V')
code('./heat --decomp serial --case V --N 64 --r 0.2 --T 0.05', bash=True)
note('Expected.', 'The program prints a single summary line. This saved example is wrapped to fit the page. Compare the step count and errors. Wall-clock time varies between runs.')
code('\n'.join(textwrap.wrap(recorded,width=95,break_long_words=False,break_on_hyphens=False)))
body('For N = 64, h = 1/64 and Δt = 0.2h². The solver takes 1,024 steps to reach the simulated time T = 0.05. The largest nodal error is about 1.03 × 10⁻⁴ and the discrete L₂ error is about 5.17 × 10⁻⁵.')
note('What to say.', '“Case V starts with a sine wave in both directions and zero edge values. We compare the computed grid with sin(πx) sin(πy) exp(−2π²t). T is simulated time. t_total and t_comp are elapsed execution times for the same serial loop, and t_comm is zero.”')

heading('3 Compare the two boundary cases', page=True)
body('Use the same N = 64, r = 0.2, and 100 steps for both runs. The only change is the prescribed boundary temperature.')
code('./heat --decomp serial --case 1 --N 64 --r 0.2 --steps 100 \\\n'
     '    --out "$demo_dir/case1.bin"\n'
     './heat --decomp serial --case 2 --N 64 --r 0.2 --steps 100 \\\n'
     '    --out "$demo_dir/case2.bin"', bash=True)
note('Expected.', 'Both summaries report P=1, N=64, r=0.2, steps=100, T=0.0048828125, and t_comm=0. Each run has equal t_total and t_comp. Only Case V reports error norms.')
body(f'Both binary fields contain 65 × 65 double values and should be {binary_size:,} bytes each.')
code('wc -c "$demo_dir/case1.bin" "$demo_dir/case2.bin"', bash=True)
p = doc.add_paragraph()
p.alignment = WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH.CENTER
p.paragraph_format.keep_with_next = True
p.paragraph_format.space_after = Pt(1)
for i in (1,2):
    if i==2:
        p.add_run('  ')
    img = p.add_run().add_picture(str(results/f'demo_case{i}.png'), width=Inches(3.18))
    img._inline.docPr.set('descr',f'Saved serial heat map for Case {i} after 100 time steps.')
doc.add_paragraph('Saved images for Case 1 and Case 2 after 100 steps. Both use the same temperature scale from 0 to 1.', 'Caption')
note('Case 1.', 'All four edges are held at 1, so the heat map has a bright border on every side.')
note('Case 2.', 'The top and bottom edges, including the corners, are held at 1. The remaining side nodes are held at 0. The heat map has two bright horizontal bands.')
note('What to say.', '“The same stencil and time step give different patterns because the edges supply heat differently. The center stays cooler than the hot edges in these short runs.”')

heading('Optional stability check',level=2)
code('./heat --decomp serial --case V --N 16 --r 0.2625 --steps 1', bash=True)
note('Expected.', 'The solver exits with an error and prints “Invalid parameters. Use N >= 2, steps >= 0, and 0 < r <= 0.25.” The requested r is 5% above the stability limit.')

heading('Regenerate the heat maps',page=True)
body('Optional. The saved heat maps on the previous page can be shown directly. To plot the new binary fields, run this block on the same Ubuntu VM after Step 3. Python 3 and Matplotlib are required.')
plot = '''python3 - "$demo_dir" <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
import struct
import sys
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

folder = Path(sys.argv[1])
width = 65
for case in (1, 2):
    source = folder / f"case{case}.bin"
    data = struct.unpack(f"<{width*width}d", source.read_bytes())
    grid = [data[j*width:(j+1)*width] for j in range(width)]
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(6, 5))
    im = ax.imshow(grid, origin="lower", extent=[0, 1, 0, 1],
                   cmap="inferno", vmin=0, vmax=1)
    ax.set(xlabel="x", ylabel="y", title=f"Case {case} after 100 steps")
    fig.colorbar(im, ax=ax, label="Temperature")
    fig.tight_layout()
    output = folder / f"case{case}.png"
    fig.savefig(output, dpi=150)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"Saved {output}")
PY'''
compile(plot.partition('\n')[2].rsplit('\n',1)[0],'<plot demo>','exec')
code(plot,bash=True)
note('Expected.', 'The script prints two Saved lines. The temporary folder now contains case1.png and case2.png, with the same color scale so you can compare them directly.')

heading('View the new plots on Windows',level=2)
body('On Ubuntu, print the actual output folder and the VM addresses.')
code('printf "%s\\n" "$demo_dir"\nhostname -I',bash=True)
body('In Windows PowerShell, replace VM_IP with the reachable heat-2 address and /tmp/ACTUAL_FOLDER with the folder printed above.')
code('scp "mpi@VM_IP:/tmp/ACTUAL_FOLDER/case*.png" "$env:USERPROFILE\\Desktop"')
body('The two PNG files are copied to your Windows Desktop.')

out = qa/'Part1_Demo_Guide.docx'
doc.save(out)
(qa/'demo_commands.sh').write_text('\n\n'.join(steps)+'\n',encoding='utf-8',newline='\n')
with ZipFile(out) as z:
    assert z.testzip() is None
    assert not any(n.startswith(('word/header','word/footer')) for n in z.namelist())
assert len(doc.inline_shapes)==2
print(out)
`;

const qa="C:/Users/User/AppData/Local/Temp/codex-part1-demo-b20fead6181c46809173bd41b9d95a9b";
const p=String.raw`
import os, struct, sys
from pathlib import Path
qa = Path(os.environ['PART1_DEMO_QA'])
trial = qa/'plot-test'
trial.mkdir()
n, width = 64, 65
for case in (1,2):
    arr = [0.0]*(width*width)
    for j in range(width):
        for i in range(width):
            if j in (0,n) or (case == 1 and i in (0,n)):
                arr[j*width+i]=1.0
    other = arr[:]
    for _ in range(100):
        for j in range(1,n):
            for i in range(1,n):
                k=j*width+i
                other[k]=0.2*arr[k]+0.2*(arr[k-1]+arr[k+1]+arr[k-width]+arr[k+width])
        arr, other = other, arr
    dest=trial/f'case{case}.bin'
    dest.write_bytes(struct.pack(f'<{len(arr)}d',*arr))
    assert dest.stat().st_size == 33800 and all(0 <= v <= 1 for v in arr)
sh=(qa/'demo_commands.sh').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
plot=sh.split("<<'PY'\n",1)[1].split('\nPY\n',1)[0]
sys.argv = ['demo_plot',str(trial)]
exec(compile(plot, 'demo_plot', 'exec'),{})
for n in (1,2):
    assert (trial/f'case{n}.png').stat().st_size > 0
print('Both plots validated with test binary fields.')

## Halo Exchange Debugging

The required small-grid tests use Case 2, N=8 and one time step.

### Two MPI processes

```bash
mpirun -np 2 ./heat_strips \
  --decomp strips --case 2 \

```

```bash
mpirun --oversubscribe -np 3 ./heat_strips \
  --decomp strips --case 2 \
  --N 8 --r 0.2 --steps 1 \
  --debug-halos
```

Both tests passed.

The three-process test verifies uneven decomposition, with three rows assigned to rank 0 and two rows assigned to each remaining rank.

Both resulting temperature fields were bit-for-bit identical to the serial reference.

## Performance Observations

For small grids, MPI communication overhead is significantly larger than computation time.

Distributed execution over Tailscale can therefore be slower than serial execution, particularly when network latency is high.

The main objective of Part 2 is numerical correctness rather than speedup.

import os
import re
from pathlib import Path
from io import BytesIO
from zipfile import ZipFile
from docx import Document
from docx.enum.table import WD_TABLE_ALIGNMENT, WD_CELL_VERTICAL_ALIGNMENT
from docx.enum.text import WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH
from docx.oxml import OxmlElement
from docx.oxml.ns import qn
from docx.shared import Inches, Pt, RGBColor

qa = Path(os.environ['PART2_REPORT_QA'])
repo = Path.cwd()
desktop = Path('C:/Users/User/Desktop')
original = desktop/'Part2_Report_Simple_Copy_Paste.docx'
with ZipFile(original) as z:
    visuals = {i:z.read(f'word/media/image{i}.png') for i in (1,2,3,4)}
logs = repo/'part2/results'
local = (logs/'step5_correctness_log.txt').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
assert len(re.findall(r'^PASS: Case=', local, re.M)) == 36
dist = (logs/'distributed_caseV_verification.txt').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
hashes = re.findall(r'^[a-f0-9]{64}',dist,re.M)
assert len(hashes) == 2 and hashes[0] == hashes[1]
assert 'N=16, P=3, r=0.2, steps=10' in dist
source = Document(original)
running = source.tables[-1]
case2_rows = [
    tuple(c.text for c in row.cells[1:])
    for row in running.rows[1:]
]
assert len(case2_rows) == 2

doc = Document()
doc.core_properties.title = 'Part 2 MPI Strip Decomposition'
doc.core_properties.author = ''
sec = doc.sections[0]
sec.page_width, sec.page_height = Inches(8.27), Inches(11.69)
sec.left_margin = sec.right_margin = Inches(.73)
sec.top_margin = sec.bottom_margin = Inches(.66)

for name, size, bold, before, after in [
    ('Normal',10.5,False,0,6),
    ('Title',16,True,0,8),
    ('Heading 1',12.5,True,8,5),
    ('Heading 2',11,True,7,4),
    ('Caption',9,False,2,5),
]:
    s=doc.styles[name]
    s.font.name='Aptos'
    s.font.size=Pt(size)
    s.font.bold=bold
    s.font.italic=False
    s.font.color.rgb=RGBColor(0,0,0)
    f=s.paragraph_format
    f.space_before,f.space_after=Pt(before),Pt(after)
    f.line_spacing=1.10 if name=='Normal' else 1.04
    f.widow_control=True
    if bold:
        f.keep_together=True
        f.keep_with_next=True
    ppr=s.element.get_or_add_pPr()
    for item in ppr.findall(qn('w:pBdr')):
        ppr.remove(item)

def body(text):
    doc.add_paragraph(text)

def heading(text,level=1,page=False):
    p=doc.add_heading(text,level)
    if page:
        p.paragraph_format.page_break_before=True

def xml(parent,name,**attrs):
    e=OxmlElement('w:'+name)
    for k,v in attrs.items():
        e.set(qn('w:'+k),str(v))
    parent.append(e)
    return e

def labeled(text,keep=False):
    p=doc.add_paragraph(text,'Caption')
    p.paragraph_format.keep_with_next=keep
    return p

def table(headers,rows,widths,caption, left_first=True):
    labeled(caption,True)
    t=doc.add_table(rows=0,cols=len(headers))
    t.alignment=WD_TABLE_ALIGNMENT.CENTER
    t.autofit=False
    for col,width in zip(t.columns,widths):
        col.width=Inches(width)
    props=t._tbl.tblPr
    borders=xml(props,'tblBorders')
    for edge in ('top','bottom','left','right','insideH','insideV'):
        xml(borders,edge,val='single',sz=4,color='D9D9D9')
    margins=xml(props,'tblCellMar')
    for edge in ('top','bottom','left','right'):
        xml(margins,edge,w=44 if edge in ('top','bottom') else 84,type='dxa')
    for ri,values in enumerate([headers,*rows]):
        row=t.add_row()
        rp=row._tr.get_or_add_trPr()
        xml(rp,'cantSplit')
        if ri==0: xml(rp,'tblHeader')
        for ci,(c,value) in enumerate(zip(row.cells,values)):
            c.width=Inches(widths[ci])
            c.vertical_alignment=WD_CELL_VERTICAL_ALIGNMENT.CENTER
            if ri==0:
                xml(c._tc.get_or_add_tcPr(),'shd',fill='F2F2F2')
            p=c.paragraphs[0]
            p.paragraph_format.space_after=Pt(0)
            p.paragraph_format.line_spacing=1.02
            p.paragraph_format.keep_with_next=ri==0
            p.alignment=WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH.LEFT if ri!=0 and ci==0 and left_first else WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH.CENTER
            r=p.add_run(str(value))
            r.bold=ri==0
            r.font.size=Pt(9.4)

def picture(which,width,caption,alt):
    p=doc.add_paragraph()
    p.alignment=WD_ALIGN_PARAGRAPH.CENTER
    p.paragraph_format.keep_with_next=True
    p.paragraph_format.space_before=Pt(4)
    p.paragraph_format.space_after=Pt(0)
    im=p.add_run().add_picture(BytesIO(visuals[which]),width=Inches(width))
    im._inline.docPr.set('descr',alt)
    labeled(caption)

commands=[]
def code(text,bash=False):
    p=doc.add_paragraph()
    p.paragraph_format.keep_together=True
    p.paragraph_format.space_after=Pt(6)
    p.paragraph_format.left_indent=Inches(.12)
    p.paragraph_format.line_spacing=1.02
    r=p.add_run(text)
    r.font.name='Consolas'
    r.font.size=Pt(9)
    if bash:
        commands.append(text)

doc.add_heading('Part 2 MPI Strip Decomposition',0)
body('We extended the serial heat solver to MPI by dividing the plate into horizontal strips. All 36 local comparisons produced the same binary temperature fields as the serial solver. The recorded two- and three-VM runs also matched their serial references.')

heading('Strip ownership and halo exchange')
body('The solver splits the N − 1 interior rows as evenly as possible. If the division leaves a remainder, the lowest ranks receive one extra row each. Rank 0 owns the bottom strip. Every rank stores its owned rows plus one ghost row above and one below. Appendix G shows the uneven split at N = 8 and P = 3.')
body('Before each update, two MPI_Sendrecv calls exchange the first and last owned rows with adjacent ranks. Each row is already contiguous, so one message carries N + 1 doubles directly from the array. MPI_PROC_NULL handles neighbors beyond the plate. Ranks then apply the same five-point stencil as the serial solver and swap the two time-level arrays.')
body('Separate blocking sends can appear to work for short rows because MPI may buffer small messages. Larger sends can wait for matching receives. If the ranks wait on one another before posting those receives, the exchange can hang. MPI_Sendrecv supplies both operations in the same call, so the matched exchanges work without relying on buffering.')

heading('Timing and output')
body('MPI_Wtime measures halo exchange and computation separately. MPI_Reduce with MPI_MAX reports the largest rank time for each metric. The maxima can come from different ranks, so the maximum computation and communication times need not sum to the maximum total. After the timed loop, MPI_Gatherv assembles the complete field on rank 0. When requested, rank 0 writes it in the same binary format as the serial solver.')

heading('Verification')
body('The local checks covered Cases 1, 2, and V, grid sizes N = 8, 10, and 16, and P = 1 through 4. One-step Case 2 tests also checked the ghost rows at P = 2 and P = 3. Distributed Case 2 passed on two and three VMs, and a 10-step Case V run passed across three VMs. Appendix H records those results. A distributed P = 4 comparison with serial is still pending for C2.')

heading('Appendix G Strip layout and local checks',page=True)
body('At N = 8, seven interior rows are split over three ranks. The first rank receives the extra row. Every rank stores two additional rows for physical boundaries or copies of neighboring owned rows.')
picture(1,6.32,'Figure G1. Uneven strip layout at N = 8 and P = 3.',
        'Seven interior rows split among three ranks, with ghost rows above and below each strip.')
table(('Rank','Global interior rows','Rows stored with ghosts'),[
    ('0','1–3','5'),('1','4–5','4'),('2','6–7','4')
],(.78,2.08,2.51),'Table G1. Row ownership and local storage.',left_first=False)
body('The one-step Case 2 checks at N = 8 printed the owned and ghost rows for P = 2 and P = 3. Both produced the same final field as serial. The automated suite used r = 0.2 and 10 steps for each of the 36 combinations.')
picture(2,5.38,'Figure G2. The local regression log reports 36 passing binary comparisons.',
        'Terminal output showing the final local regression cases and a total of 36 passing tests.')
body('Source log: part2/results/step5_correctness_log.txt. This suite runs on one VM. Its results verify correctness and provide no distributed performance measurements.')

heading('Appendix H Distributed verification',page=True)
body('The two- and three-VM Case 2 runs used N = 8, r = 0.2, and 10 steps. The three-VM Case V run used N = 16, r = 0.2, and 10 steps. Each binary output matched the corresponding serial field.')
table(('Test','Ranks','Total (s)','Compute (s)','Comm. (s)'),[
    ('Case 2, two VMs','2',*case2_rows[0]),
    ('Case 2, three VMs','3',*case2_rows[1]),
    ('Case V, three VMs','3','0.779550588','0.000008195','0.779543372')
],(1.53,.63,1.30,1.30,1.30),'Table H1. Timed loop maxima for the recorded distributed runs.')
picture(3,5.70,'Figure H1. Two-VM Case 2 on heat-2 and heat-1 passed cmp.',
        'Ubuntu terminal screenshot showing the two-VM Case 2 run and the matching serial output.')
picture(4,5.70,'Figure H2. Three-VM Case 2 passed cmp.',
        'Ubuntu terminal screenshot showing the three-VM Case 2 run and the matching serial output.')
body('The 10-step Case V run recorded identical SHA-256 hashes for serial and MPI, with a maximum error of 0.000598805794 and a discrete L₂ error of 0.000299402897 in both runs. The earlier 64-step attempt was interrupted. The short test is recorded in part2/results/distributed_caseV_verification.txt.')
body('The two- and three-VM tests demonstrate correctness for the listed settings. The full distributed C2 coverage is pending. In particular, the available P = 4 comparisons were local. The timings illustrate communication cost on these small tests.')

heading('Appendix I Reproducing the Part 2 checks',page=True)
body('Run these commands in Bash as mpi on Ubuntu. The first block assumes the serial and strips source directories are side by side under the home directory.')
code('cd ~/part2\n'
     'make -C ../part1\n'
     'make\n'
     'python3 tests/check_decomposition.py\n'
     'bash tests/check_correctness.sh',bash=True)
body('The local suite should finish with TOTAL: 36/36 tests passed. It allows local oversubscription to check more ranks than a VM has cores.')
heading('Inspect the ghost rows',level=2)
code('for p in 2 3; do\n'
     '    mpirun --oversubscribe -np "$p" ./heat_strips \\\n'
     '        --decomp strips --case 2 --N 8 --r 0.2 --steps 1 --debug-halos\n'
     'done',bash=True)
body('The P = 2 run splits the interior rows 4 and 3. The P = 3 run splits them 3, 2, and 2. The diagnostic prints the local arrays, including ghost rows, after the first exchange.')
heading('Repeat the recorded three-VM Case V comparison',level=2)
body('On heat-2, use the hosts_3vm file described in the Part 2 README, with heat-2 listed first. Build heat_strips at the same path on all three VMs. This block saves the new fields in a temporary folder on heat-2.')
code('run_dir=$(mktemp -d)\n'
     'timeout 60s mpirun --hostfile hosts_3vm -np 3 --nooversubscribe --map-by slot \\\n'
     '    --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \\\n'
     '    --mca btl tcp,self ./heat_strips --decomp strips --case V \\\n'

body('When both runs complete, a passing comparison prints PASS: fields match. These commands repeat the documented P = 3 experiment.')
body('Sources. Project specification, Section 6. Numerical Methods for the Heat Equation, Chapter 8. Part 2 source, test logs, and the recorded two- and three-VM terminal evidence.')

out=qa/'Part2_Report_Copy_Ready.docx'

SHA256 38ccc8d66a8a060ac298f360bd6e77c0d032470bccd2b9ef08544d134d50d925
sections 1 paragraphs 140 tables 6 inline figures 16
LABEL 3 Part 0: Building the cluster
LABEL 16 Part 1: Serial heat solver and verification
LABEL 30 Part 2: MPI Strip Decomposition
PART2 30 Part 2: MPI Strip Decomposition 
PART2 31 Strip ownership and halo exchange 
PART2 32 The solver splits the N − 1 interior rows as evenly as possible. If the division leaves a remainder, the lowest ranks receive one extra row each. Rank 0 owns the bottom strip. Every rank stores its owned rows plus one ghost row above and one below. Appendix G shows the uneven split at N = 8 and P = 3. 
PART2 33 Before each update, two MPI_Sendrecv calls exchange the first and last owned rows with adjacent ranks. Each row is already contiguous, so one message carries N + 1 doubles directly from the array. MPI_PROC_NULL handles neighbors beyond the plate. Ranks then apply the same five-point stencil as the serial solver and swap the two time-level arrays. 
PART2 34 Separate blocking sends can appear to work for short rows because MPI may buffer small messages. Larger sends can wait for matching receives. If the ranks wait on one another before posting those receives, the exchange can hang. MPI_Sendrecv supplies both operations in the same call, so the matched exchanges work without relying on buffering. 
PART2 35 Timing and output 
PART2 36 MPI_Wtime measures halo exchange and computation separately. MPI_Reduce with MPI_MAX reports the largest rank time for each metric. The maxima can come from different ranks, so the maximum computation and communication times need not sum to the maximum total. After the timed loop, MPI_Gatherv assembles the complete field on rank 0. When requested, rank 0 writes it in the same binary format as the serial solver. 
PART2 37 Verification 
PART2 38 The local checks covered Cases 1, 2, and V, grid sizes N = 8, 10, and 16, and P = 1 through 4. One-step Case 2 tests also checked the ghost rows at P = 2 and P = 3. ### 🤖 Codex (2026-11-25 14:21:02)

To mark **Part 2 DONE**, finish the missing C2 checks on **heat-2 and heat-1**. You already have passing results for \(N=8,\ P=2\) and \(P=3\). Run the four remaining comparisons below and keep the log.

### 1. Build the strips solver on both VMs

Run this in **Ubuntu Bash on heat-1 and heat-2**, using the `mpi` account and the same Part 2 source on both machines.

```bash
cd ~/part2
make
```

### 2. Run the missing comparisons from heat-2

Stay in **Ubuntu Bash on heat-2**. First confirm that heat-1 is reachable over Tailscale and that SSH returns `heat-1` without prompting for a password.

```bash
tailscale ping heat-1
ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=5 mpi@100.100.125.10 hostname
```

Then paste this block into the same terminal. It uses **Case 2, \(r=0.2\), and 10 steps** for both the serial and MPI runs. It creates a new results folder so your earlier tests are preserved.

```bash
cd ~/part2
make -C ../part1
mkdir -p results
run_dir=$(mktemp -d "$PWD/results/c2_XXXXXX")

cat > "$run_dir/hosts" <<'EOF'
100.98.24.109 slots=2 max_slots=2
100.100.125.10 slots=2 max_slots=2
EOF

(
    set -euo pipefail
    date -Is

    for n in 8 10; do
        ../part1/heat --decomp serial --case 2 --N "$n" \
            --r 0.2 --steps 10 --out "$run_dir/serial_N${n}.bin"
    done

    for config in 8:4 10:2 10:3 10:4; do
        n=${config%:*}
        p=${config#*:}
        field="$run_dir/strips_N${n}_P${p}.bin"

        timeout 60s mpirun --hostfile "$run_dir/hosts" \
            -np "$p" --map-by node --nooversubscribe --display-map \
            --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
            --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
            --mca btl tcp,self \
            ./heat_strips --decomp strips --case 2 --N "$n" \
            --r 0.2 --steps 10 --out "$field"

        cmp "$run_dir/serial_N${n}.bin" "$field"
        echo "PASS: N=$n P=$p matches serial byte for byte"
    done
) 2>&1 | tee "$run_dir/verification.log"

echo "Log saved at $run_dir/verification.log"
```

### 3. Confirm the evidence before marking DONE

The log should contain these four lines. For each \(P=4\) run, the process map should show **two ranks on heat-2 and two on heat-1**.

```text
PASS: N=8 P=4 matches serial byte for byte
PASS: N=10 P=2 matches serial byte for byte
PASS: N=10 P=3 matches serial byte for byte
PASS: N=10 P=4 matches serial byte for byte
```

Once all four pass, save `verification.log` with the Part 2 results and update the **pending C2 wording in the Part 2 body and Appendix G**. You can then mark **Part 2 DONE**.

from docx import Document
root=Path('part2/results')

 if 'checkpoint_c2' in str(p) and p.is_file():print(p,p.stat().st_size)
for n in (8,10):

 b=(root/'checkpoint_c2_ali'/f'strips_N{n}_P4.bin').read_bytes()
 assert a==b and len(a)==8*(n+1)**2

 print('VERIFIED',n,len(a),hashlib.sha256(a).hexdigest())
 for role in ['serial', 'strips']:
  name=f'serial_N{n}.log' if role=='serial' else f'strips_N{n}_P4.log'
  text=(root/'checkpoint_c2_ali'/name).read_text()
  line=next(l for l in text.splitlines() if l.startswith('decomp='))

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: part2/.gitignore\n@@\n *.bin\n+!/results/checkpoint_c2_ali/serial_N8.bin\n+!/results/checkpoint_c2_ali/strips_N8_P4.bin\n+!/results/checkpoint_c2_ali/serial_N10.bin\n+!/results/checkpoint_c2_ali/strips_N10_P4.bin\n*** End Patch"));
const section=String.raw`## Checkpoint C2 completed

On November 28, 2026, Case 2 passed distributed P=4 checks at N=8 and N=10 over heat-2 and heat-1. Both runs used r=0.2 and 10 steps. Independent byte-for-byte comparisons of the retained fields confirmed the recorded cmp results.

The [hostfile](hosts_c2_ali) assigns two slots to each VM. Slot placement puts ranks 0 and 1 on heat-2 (100.98.24.109) and ranks 2 and 3 on heat-1 (100.100.125.10). The [rank map](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/rank_placement.log) and both solver logs confirm that allocation. The run configuration selected TCP over Tailscale between VMs and enabled the vader shared-memory transport within a VM. These transport settings were recorded by the team.

| N | Interior rows per rank | Serial (s) | MPI total (s) | MPI compute (s) | MPI communication (s) | Field comparison |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 8 | 2, 2, 2, 1 | 0.000001766 | 1.230770150 | 0.000005149 | 1.230758227 | PASS |
| 10 | 3, 2, 2, 2 | 0.000002542 | 0.773958649 | 0.000004584 | 0.773950409 | PASS |

Both decompositions are uneven because N-1 is not divisible by four. The team recorded exit code 0 for both runs. The saved summaries and complete matching output fields confirm successful completion. These are correctness tests. Performance comparisons remain in Part 4.

### Saved evidence

- N=8 [serial summary](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/serial_N8.log), [MPI summary and rank map](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/strips_N8_P4.log), and [cmp result](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/compare_N8_P4.log).
- N=10 [serial summary](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/serial_N10.log), [MPI summary and rank map](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/strips_N10_P4.log), and [cmp result](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/compare_N10_P4.log).
- N=8 retained [serial field](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/serial_N8.bin) and [MPI field](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/strips_N8_P4.bin), each 648 bytes, SHA-256 `6db3254208dd3322257f29ac684c2df596df50a9bc1869f867937e7132f2cba9`.
- N=10 retained [serial field](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/serial_N10.bin) and [MPI field](results/checkpoint_c2_ali/strips_N10_P4.bin), each 968 bytes, SHA-256 `6f5187f409defda578b0e41e5c76d1ed3ba31efd6fe768fedcd0da5fc0f21214`.

### Reproduce the distributed P=4 comparisons

On Ubuntu heat-2, run from ~/part2 after compiling heat_strips at the same path on both VMs. Build the serial reference in ../part1. This creates a new output directory.

```bash
make -C ../part1
make
run_dir=$(mktemp -d "$PWD/results/c2_repeat_XXXXXX")
for n in 8 10; do
    ../part1/heat --decomp serial --case 2 --N "$n" --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
        --out "$run_dir/serial_N${n}.bin" > "$run_dir/serial_N${n}.log"
    if timeout --kill-after=5s 60s mpirun --hostfile hosts_c2_ali -np 4 \
        --map-by slot --nooversubscribe --display-map \
        --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
        --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 --mca btl vader,tcp,self \
        ./heat_strips --decomp strips --case 2 --N "$n" --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
        --out "$run_dir/strips_N${n}_P4.bin" \
        > "$run_dir/strips_N${n}_P4.log" 2>&1
    then
        cmp "$run_dir/serial_N${n}.bin" "$run_dir/strips_N${n}_P4.bin" \
            && echo "PASS: N=$n P=4 fields match" || break
    else
        echo "MPI run failed. Inspect $run_dir/strips_N${n}_P4.log"
        break
    fi
done
```

The existing 36 local comparisons, previous two- and three-VM checks, and these distributed P=4 checks complete the planned C2 technical verification. Professor checkpoint approval is recorded separately by the course.

### Earlier heat-3 attempts

The [heat-2 / heat-3 hostfile](hosts_c2) and [attempt evidence](results/checkpoint_c2/) are retained. The [rank map](results/checkpoint_c2/rank_placement.log) confirms process launching. The empty [MPI output](results/checkpoint_c2/strips_N8_P4.log) and [post-restart map](results/checkpoint_c2/rank_placement_after_restart.log) provide no completed solver result. The team reported frozen MPI execution and Linux kernel RCU CPU stall warnings on heat-3. Those warnings are team observations and were not captured in these files. The cause remains unconfirmed. The later heat-1 runs completed successfully.

    field="$run_dir/strips_N$n.bin"
    ../part1/heat --decomp serial --case 2 --N "$n" --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
        --out "$serial" > "$run_dir/serial_N$n.log"
    if timeout --kill-after=5s 60s mpirun --hostfile hosts_c2_ali -np 4 \
        --map-by slot --nooversubscribe --display-map \
        --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
        --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 --mca btl vader,tcp,self \
        ./heat_strips --decomp strips --case 2 --N "$n" --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
        --out "$field" > "$run_dir/strips_N$n.log" 2>&1
    then
        cmp "$serial" "$field" && echo "PASS: N=$n P=4 fields match" || break
    else
        echo "MPI run failed. Inspect $run_dir/strips_N$n.log"
        break
    fi
done
~~~

The 36 local comparisons, previous two- and three-VM checks, and these distributed P=4 checks complete the planned C2 technical verification. Professor checkpoint approval is recorded separately by the course.

### Earlier heat-3 attempts

The [heat-2 / heat-3 hostfile](hosts_c2) and [attempt evidence](results/checkpoint_c2/) are retained. The [rank map](results/checkpoint_c2/rank_placement.log) confirms process launching. The empty [MPI output](results/checkpoint_c2/strips_N8_P4.log) and [post-restart map](results/checkpoint_c2/rank_placement_after_restart.log) provide no completed solver result. The team reported frozen MPI execution and Linux kernel RCU CPU stall warnings on heat-3. Those warnings were not captured in these files. The cause remains unconfirmed. The later heat-1 runs completed successfully.

Each team also submits a presentation of at most three slides. The first slide presents your infrastructure and what it can deliver: the hardware of each node (CPU cores, memory and memory bandwidth, and GPU if any), the latency and bandwidth you measured between nodes, and, from these figures, an estimate of the best possible time per step on a single node and a prediction of whether computation or communication should dominate. The second slide covers the discretization and the compute model your update rule and stability limit, how the grid is split into strips and blocks with their ghost layers, what a single time step does, and your performance model. It ends with one line of correctness evidence: the convergence order you observed and the confirmation that your parallel output is bit-identical to the serial one. The third slide presents your results and what you learned: the measured speed-up against your model's prediction, the grid size beyond which distributing the work pays off, the share of time spent communicating. do we need that report?

#!/usr/bin/env bash

set -euo pipefail

cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."

SERIAL="../part1/heat"
BLOCKS="./heat_blocks"

mkdir -p results

TMP_DIR=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$TMP_DIR"' EXIT

PASSED=0
FAILED=0
TOTAL=0

echo "=========================================="
echo "Part 3 - Automated Correctness Tests"
echo "=========================================="

for CASE in 1 2 V; do
    for N in 8 9 10 16; do

        SERIAL_OUT="$TMP_DIR/serial.bin"

        "$SERIAL" \
            --decomp serial \
            --case "$CASE" \
            --N "$N" \
            --r 0.2 \
            --steps 10 \
            --out "$SERIAL_OUT" > /dev/null

        for P in 1 2 3 4 6 8; do

            TOTAL=$((TOTAL + 1))

            MPI_OUT="$TMP_DIR/blocks.bin"

            rm -f "$MPI_OUT"

            if timeout 30s mpirun --oversubscribe \
                -np "$P" "$BLOCKS" \
                --decomp blocks \
                --case "$CASE" \
                --N "$N" \
                --r 0.2 \
                --steps 10 \
                --out "$MPI_OUT" > /dev/null \
                && cmp -s "$SERIAL_OUT" "$MPI_OUT"; then

                echo "PASS: Case=$CASE N=$N P=$P"

                PASSED=$((PASSED + 1))

            else

                echo "FAIL: Case=$CASE N=$N P=$P"

--map-by slot \\\\\n\n&#x20; --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \\\\\n\n&#x20; --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \\\\\n\n&#x20; --mca btl tcp,self \\\\\n\n&#x20; ./heat_blocks --decomp blocks --case 2 \\\\\n\n&#x20; --N 16 --r 0.2 --steps 10 \\\\\n\n&#x20; --out results/case2.bin\n\n```\n\nUpdate the hostfiles according to the available machines and their Tailscale addresses.\n\nThe executable must be compiled and available at the same path on every participating VM.\n\n## Simulation cases\n\n- Case 1: Four hot boundaries.\n\n- Case 2: Hot top and bottom boundaries and cold lateral boundaries, with hot corners.\n\n- Case V: Analytical verification using a sinusoidal initial temperature and zero boundary temperatures.\n\n## Correctness verification\n\nThe automated correctness tests cover:\n\n- Grid sizes N = 8, 9, 10 and 16.\n\n- Process counts P = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8.\n\n- All three simulation cases.\n\nResult: 72/72 tests passed.\n\nDistributed execution was also verified using multiple VMs. The results were identical to the serial reference in the tested configurations.\n\nTo run the automated tests:\n\n```bash\n\nbash tests/check_correctness.sh\n\n```\n\nCompile the Part 1 reference solver before running the tests. Local tests with more processes than available CPU slots use oversubscription and are not performance benchmarks.\n\n## Simulation results\n\n### Case 1\n\n![Case 1](results/demo_blocks_case1.png)\n\n### Case 2\n\n![Case 2](results/demo_blocks_case2.png)\n\n### Case V\n\n![Case V](results/demo_blocks_caseV.png)\n\nThese demonstrations use N = 16, P = 4, r = 0.2 and 10 time steps.\n\n## Performance comparison\n\nThe performance experiments compare the 1D strip and 2D block implementations.\n\nExperimental configuration:\n\n- Two Ubuntu VMs connected through Tailscale.\n\n- Four MPI processes.\n\n- Case 2.\n\n- r = 0.24.\n\n- 10 time steps.\n\n- Three repetitions per method and grid size.\n\n### Median execution times\n\n| Grid size | Processes | 1D Strips (s) | 2D Blocks (s) | Strips/Blocks ratio |\n\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n\n| 512 | 4 | 3.3820 | 2.0395 | 1.66 |\n\n| 2048 | 4 | 6.9643 | 4.4194 | 1.58 |\n\n| 8192 | 4 | Not completed | Not completed | N/A |\n\nThe 2D block decomposition achieved lower median execution times for both completed grid sizes.\n\nCommunication dominated the execution time because the processes communicated across the Tailscale network.\n\nThe results support the theoretical prediction for the tested P = 4 configurations. However, network variability caused significant differences between individual repetitions.\n\n## Benchmark execution\n\n```bash\n\nbash benchmarks/compare_2vm.sh pilot\n\n```\n\nTo attempt the complete two-VM benchmark:\n\n```bash\n\nbash benchmarks/compare_2vm.sh full\n\n```\n\nThe Part 2 strip solver must also be compiled and available on the participating machines. The benchmark scripts assume the project directories are located under the MPI user's home directory.\n\nThe raw logs of the 12 successful benchmark runs are available in `results/final_benchmarks/`.\n\n## Experimental limitations\n\nThe N = 8192 benchmarks were attempted several times but could not be completed because of excessive execution times and recurring Tailscale connectivity failures.\n\nExperimental performance measurements with P = 8 were not performed because the three available VMs provided only six virtual CPUs in total.\n\nOversubscription was avoided during performance experiments.\n\nThe theoretical analysis includes the missing configurations, but no experimental results are claimed for them.\n\n## Project structure\n\n```text\n\npart3/\n\n├── src/\n\n│   └── blocks.cpp\n\n├── tests/\n\n├── benchmarks/\n\n├── results/\n\n│   ├── final_benchmarks/\n\n│   ├── demo_blocks_case1.png\n\n│   ├── demo_blocks_case2.png\n\n│   ├── demo_blocks_caseV.png\n\n│   ├── demo_verification.txt\n\n│   └── step16_correctness_log.txt\n\n├── Makefile\n\n├── .gitignore\n\n├── README.md\n\n└── MPI hostfiles\n\n```\n\n## Conclusion\n\nThe MPI 2D block implementation successfully reproduces the serial reference solution for the tested configurations.\n\nThe completed performance experiments demonstrate the potential advantage of 2D block decomposition over 1D strips, while highlighting the impact of network latency and connectivity on distributed performance.\n\r\n"}

out=[]

 if line.startswith(fence):
  in_code=not in_code
  out.append(line)
 elif in_code:
  if line.strip():
   if line.endswith(chr(92)*2):line=line[:-1]

  out.append(line)
 else:out.append(line)
s='\n'.join(out)+'\n'
assert '&#' not in s

## Computer Use

Control native apps and browsers on the user’s computer by reading or operating UI. Prefer purpose-built connectors, APIs, or CLIs when available.

- Use `cua_repl` (JavaScript) for all UI actions.
- Do not use other technologies besides `cua_repl` for computer interactions, unless specifically requested by the user (e.g. AppleScript, `osascript`, JXA, System Events, CGEvent synthesis).
- Prefer a dedicated plugin or skill when it can complete the task; use Computer Use for interactions that are not exposed through a more specific interface.
- `cua_repl` state is persistent across calls
- If you create a tab or get an app, the initial UI state is automatically included in the tool result.

## API

```typescript
type Vec2 = [x: number, y: number];
type ObservationOptions = { emit?: boolean };
type StateOptions = ObservationOptions & { disableDiffing?: boolean };
type ScreenshotRegion = {
  id: string;
  zIndex: number;
  bytes: Uint8Array;
  originX?: number;
  originY?: number;
  width?: number;
  height?: number;
};
type StateAndScreenshot = {
  state: string;
  screenshot?: Uint8Array;
  screenshots?: ScreenshotRegion[];
};
type ScreenshotInputOptions = { screenshotId?: string }; // Windows only
type AppClickOptions = ClickOptions & ScreenshotInputOptions;
type AppScrollOptions = { pixels: number } & ScreenshotInputOptions;
type PasteOptions = { format?: "text" | "md" | "html" };
type ClickOptions = {
  mouseButton?: MouseButton;
  clickCount?: number;
  key?: string;
  durationMs?: number;
};
type PressKeyOptions = { durationMs?: number };
type SelectTextOptions = {
  prefix?: string;
  suffix?: string;
  selectionType?: SelectionType;
};
type Direction = "up" | "down" | "left" | "right" | "u" | "d" | "l" | "r";
type SelectionType = "text" | "cursor_before" | "cursor_after";
type MouseButton = "left" | "right" | "middle" | "l" | "r" | "m";

interface Target<ScreenshotResult = Uint8Array | ScreenshotRegion[]> {
  getAXState(options?: StateOptions): Promise<string>;
  getScreenshot(options?: ObservationOptions): Promise<ScreenshotResult>;
  getAXStateAndScreenshot(options?: StateOptions): Promise<StateAndScreenshot>;
  click(target: number | Vec2, options?: ClickOptions): Promise<void>;
  drag(from: Vec2, to: Vec2): Promise<void>;
  scroll(target: number | Vec2, direction: Direction, pages?: number): Promise<void>;
  selectText(elementIndex: number, text: string, options?: SelectTextOptions): Promise<void>;
  setValue(elementIndex: number, value: string): Promise<void>;
  performSecondaryAction(elementIndex: number, action: string): Promise<void>;
}

type AppInfo = {
  id: string;
  displayName?: string;
  lastUsedDate?: string;
  useCount?: number;
  isRunning?: boolean;
  windows?: WindowInfo[];
};
type WindowInfo = { id: number | string; app: string; title?: string; owner?: string };

interface App extends Target {
  click(target: number | Vec2, options?: AppClickOptions): Promise<void>;
  drag(from: Vec2, to: Vec2, options?: ScreenshotInputOptions): Promise<void>;
  scroll(
    target: number | Vec2,
    direction: Direction,
    distance?: number | AppScrollOptions,
  ): Promise<void>;
  paste(text: string, options?: PasteOptions): Promise<void>;
  pressKey(key: string, options?: PressKeyOptions): Promise<void>;
  // Type at current focus, or pass a field index from getAXState() to target it (Linux).
  typeText(text: string, elementIndex?: number): Promise<void>;
  // Send printable keys to current focus without pasting or finding a text field (Linux only).
  typeCharacters?(text: string): Promise<{ charactersSent: number }>;
}

type BrowserInfo = {
  id: string;
  name?: string;
  family?: string;
  type?: "iab" | "extension" | "cdp" | "mcpapps";
  profileName?: string;
  metadata?: { extensionInstanceId?: string; codexSessionId?: string };
};

type BrowserTabInfo = {
  id: string;
  providerTabId?: string;
  title?: string;
  url?: string;
};

interface Browser {
  readonly browserId: string;
  documentation(): Promise<string>;
}

interface BrowserProvider {
  list(): Promise<BrowserInfo[]>;
  get(id: string): Promise<Browser>;
}

interface BrowserState extends BrowserInfo {
  tabs: BrowserTabInfo[];
}

type TabInfo = {
  id: string;
  providerTabId?: string;
  browserId: string;
  title?: string;
  url?: string;
};

type State = {
  apps: AppInfo[];
  browsers: BrowserState[];
  errors?: string[]; // Inventory failures; the other inventory remains usable.
};

type BrowserOptions = { browser?: string };
type GetBrowserOptions = { id?: string; extensionInstanceId?: string; url?: string };
type CreateBrowserTabOptions = { visible?: boolean; sessionName?: string };

/** Native input wrappers throw on DOM-only tabs. Use documented Playwright locators instead. */
interface Tab extends Target<Uint8Array> {

  markHandoff?(): Promise<void>;
}

  listWindows?(options?: ObservationOptions): P

# Part 3 – MPI 2D Block Decomposition

## Objective

Implement a parallel 2D heat diffusion solver using MPI with a two-dimensional block decomposition and compare its correctness and performance with the serial and 1D strip implementations.

## Implementation

The solver uses:

- MPI_Dims_create to determine the process grid dimensions.
- MPI_Cart_create to create a non-periodic Cartesian topology.
- MPI_Cart_shift to identify neighboring processes.
- Balanced distribution of interior grid points.
- Ghost cells on all four sides of each local block.
- MPI_Type_vector for non-contiguous column communication.
- MPI_Sendrecv for halo exchanges.
- MPI_Gatherv for global grid reconstruction.
- MPI_Wtime for computation and communication measurements.

Corner ghost cells are not exchanged because the five-point stencil does not require diagonal neighbors.

### Why corners are not needed

The five-point update of node (i, j) reads only (i, j) and its four edge
neighbours (i±1, j) and (i, j±1). For a node in the corner of a block, those
neighbours lie in the left/right ghost column or the bottom/top ghost row, never
in a corner ghost cell. A stencil that reads diagonal neighbours would need the
corners, for example the nine-point (compact) Laplacian, which uses
(i±1, j±1). With it, the corner values would have to be exchanged too: either
four extra diagonal messages, or by doing the row exchange first and then sending
columns that include the freshly received ghost rows.

## Compilation

```bash
cd part3
make
```

The generated executable is `heat_blocks`.

## Execution

### Local execution

```bash
mpirun -np 4 ./heat_blocks --decomp blocks --case 2 --N 16 --r 0.2 --steps 10
```

### Distributed execution over Tailscale

```bash
mpirun --hostfile hosts_3vm_P4 -np 4 \
  --map-by slot \
  --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \
  --mca btl tcp,self \
  ./heat_blocks --decomp blocks --case 2 \
  --N 16 --r 0.2 --steps 10 \
  --out results/case2.bin
```

Update the hostfiles according to the available machines and their Tailscale addresses.

The executable must be compiled and available at the same path on every participating VM.

## Simulation cases

- Case 1: Four hot boundaries.
- Case 2: Hot top and bottom boundaries and cold lateral boundaries, with hot corners.
- Case V: Analytical verification using a sinusoidal initial temperature and zero boundary temperatures.

## Correctness verification

The automated correctness tests cover:

- Grid sizes N = 8, 9, 10 and 16.
- Process counts P = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8.
- All three simulation cases.

Result: 72/72 tests passed.

Distributed execution was also verified using multiple VMs. The results were identical to the serial reference in the tested configurations.

To run the automated tests:

```bash
bash tests/check_correctness.sh
```

Compile the Part 1 reference solver before running the tests. Local tests with more processes than available CPU slots use oversubscription and are not performance benchmarks.

## Simulation results

### Case 1

![Case 1](results/demo_blocks_case1.png)

### Case 2

![Case 2](results/demo_blocks_case2.png)

### Case V

![Case V](results/demo_blocks_caseV.png)

These demonstrations use N = 16, P = 4, r = 0.2 and 10 time steps.

## Prediction: strips or blocks? (Task 8.2)

Computed by `analysis/predict_strips_vs_blocks.py` from the Part 0 network
measurements (heat-2 ↔ heat-1: ts = 3.511 ms, 1.746 MB/s) and the Part 4
per-VM compute cost. Output: `results/predictions_strips_vs_blocks.csv`.

- **Simple model** (one interior process, every message on the cross-VM link):
  strips Tcomm = 2(ts + (N+1)tw), blocks Tcomm = 4(ts + (N/√P)tw).
- **Refined model** (slot placement, real block sizes and physical edges,
  same-VM vs cross-VM links, per-VM compute speed; slowest rank per step).
  P = 4 is heat-2 ×2 + heat-1 ×2; P = 8 is heat-2 ×4 + heat-1 ×4.

| P | N | Simple: strips comm (ms) | Simple: blocks comm (ms) | Refined: strips step (ms) | Refi

def classify(path):
    """Return (source, hostfile_hosts, mapping, status, reason)."""
    rel = path.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix()
    parts = path.relative_to(ROOT).parts

    if "consolidated" in parts or path.name == "campaign.log":
        return None
    if any(p.startswith("serial_") for p in parts):
        return None  # serial baseline is handled by serial_summary.csv

    # New campaign folders: placement is recorded in meta.txt.
    for i, p in enumerate(parts):
        if p.startswith("campaign_"):
            meta_path = path.parent / "meta.txt"

Objective\r\n\r\nImplement a parallel 2D heat diffusion solver using MPI with a two-dimensional block decomposition and compare its correctness and performance with the serial and 1D strip implementations.\r\n\r\n## Implementation\r\n\r\nThe solver uses:\r\n\r\n- MPI_Dims_create to determine the process grid dimensions.\r\n- MPI_Cart_create to create a non-periodic Cartesian topology.\r\n- MPI_Cart_shift to identify neighboring processes.\r\n- Balanced distribution of interior grid points.\r\n- Ghost cells on all four sides of each local block.\r\n- MPI_Type_vector for non-contiguous column communication.\r\n- MPI_Sendrecv for halo exchanges.\r\n- MPI_Gatherv for global grid reconstruction.\r\n- MPI_Wtime for computation and communication measurements.\r\n\r\nCorner ghost cells are not exchanged because the five-point stencil does not require diagonal neighbors.\r\n\r\n### Why corners are not needed\r\n\r\nThe five-point update of node (i, j) reads only (i, j) and its four edge\r\nneighbours (i±1, j) and (i, j±1). For a node in the corner of a block, those\r\nneighbours lie in the left/right ghost column or the bottom/top ghost row, never\r\nin a corner ghost cell. A stencil that reads diagonal neighbours would need the\r\ncorners, for example the nine-point (compact) Laplacian, which uses\r\n(i±1, j±1). With it, the corner values would have to be exchanged too: either\r\nfour extra diagonal messages, or by doing the row exchange first and then sending\r\ncolumns that include the freshly received ghost rows.\r\n\r\n## Compilation\r\n\r\n```bash\r\ncd part3\r\nmake\r\n```\r\n\r\nThe generated executable is `heat_blocks`.\r\n\r\n## Execution\r\n\r\n### Local execution\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nmpirun -np 4 ./heat_blocks --decomp blocks --case 2 --N 16 --r 0.2 --steps 10\r\n```\r\n\r\n### Distributed execution over Tailscale\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nmpirun --hostfile hosts_3vm_P4 -np 4 \\\r\n  --map-by slot \\\r\n  --mca oob_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \\\r\n  --mca btl_tcp_if_include tailscale0 \\\r\n  --mca btl tcp,self \\\r\n  ./heat_blocks --decomp blocks --case 2 \\\r\n  --N 16 --r 0.2 --steps 10 \\\r\n  --out results/case2.bin\r\n```\r\n\r\nUpdate the hostfiles according to the available machines and their Tailscale addresses.\r\n\r\nThe executable must be compiled and available at the same path on every participating VM.\r\n\r\n## Simulation cases\r\n\r\n- Case 1: Four hot boundaries.\r\n- Case 2: Hot top and bottom boundaries and cold lateral boundaries, with hot corners.\r\n- Case V: Analytical verification using a sinusoidal initial temperature and zero boundary temperatures.\r\n\r\n## Correctness verification\r\n\r\nThe automated correctness tests cover:\r\n\r\n- Grid sizes N = 8, 9, 10 and 16.\r\n- Process counts P = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8.\r\n- All three simulation cases.\r\n\r\nResult: 72/72 tests passed.\r\n\r\nDistributed execution was also verified using multiple VMs. The results were identical to the serial reference in the tested configurations.\r\n\r\nTo run the automated tests:\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nbash tests/check_correctness.sh\r\n```\r\n\r\nCompile the Part 1 reference solver before running the tests. Blocks were faster at every size, with a larger advantage on the largest grid. The ratios compare the two parallel methods; they are not speed-ups against a serial baseline.')\nhead('Performance model and prediction')\npara('For a message of m double values, the link model is tₛ + mtw. The refined model uses actual block sizes, physical edges, slot placement, different local and cross-VM links, and the measured compute cost on each VM.
